    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I don’t really feel like myself anymore. I used to be pretty active, you know—hiking, coaching, playing guitar. I’d call people back. I was the kind of guy who kept moving.\n\nNow I just… don’t. Some days I sit in that little rental place for hours, and I know there are things I should do—basic stuff—but I can’t get myself to start. It’s strange, because I can see the day going by, like the light changes in the room and everything, and I’m just still sitting there. I don’t know. It’s not how I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. I mean, hiking used to be my thing. I’d get up early, pack too much coffee, complain about my knees, all that. And coaching too—I liked being around the kids, keeping them focused. Guitar, even just sitting there messing around with chords.\n\nNow it’s like… I look at the guitar and it’s just wood and strings. I don’t call people back. I tell myself I will, then the phone’s sitting there and I just don’t. Some days I’m in that little rental for hours, knowing there’s stuff I should do, and I can’t make myself start. It’s not even that I decided I don’t like those things anymore. It’s more like I’m not really there for them.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for a period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it depressed like that. I mean… I have days where I’m just kind of sitting there and everything feels far away, but “most of the day, nearly every day”—I’m not sure how to measure that.\n\nSometimes people say depressed and they mean crying or, you know, like my cousin after his divorce, he had this whole routine with sleeping on the couch and ordering the same noodles every night. I’m not really… I don’t know. I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel down or in a low mood throughout the day? Does it tend to last most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to pin it down like that. I wouldn’t want to say “most of the day nearly every day” if that’s a specific thing you’re asking.\n\nI do have stretches where I feel low, or kind of… flattened out, I guess. But then sometimes I’m not really thinking “I’m sad,” I’m just sitting there, and the day gets away from me. Like I’ll notice the neighbor’s dog barking or the mail truck, and then I’m thinking about how my old route used to have this one house with three dogs that all had different barks—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track hour by hour. It comes and goes, maybe, or I just don’t notice it the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, for most of the day, you feel down or in a low mood?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that exactly. “Most days, most of the day” feels… too neat, I guess. I have low parts, yeah, or blank parts, but I don’t always know what to call them.\n\nLike yesterday I sat by the window and watched this guy try to parallel park for, I don’t know, ten minutes, and I was thinking about how people in the city just keep tapping bumpers like it’s normal. And then suddenly it was late. Was I down that whole time? I don’t know. I wasn’t happy, but I wasn’t sitting there thinking, “I’m sad,” either.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I don’t think I can give you a clean yes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or in a low mood for most of the day, nearly every day, even if it's not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, but I still don’t really know how to answer it cleanly. Like, “low mood” could mean a lot of things. Sometimes I’m quiet for hours, but I might just be watching the light move on the wall or thinking about whether I left something on the counter.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean I’m definitely down most of the day, nearly every day. I just… I haven’t been measuring it like that. It’s more like the day gets kind of dull or heavy sometimes, but I can’t tell if that counts.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or lack energy, even if you haven’t done much during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not always like tired from doing something, though. It’s more like I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike, and I coached, played guitar sometimes… I’d call people back. Now I’ll be in that little rental place and there are things I know I should do, like basic stuff, and I just sit there for hours. Not even exactly sleeping. Just sitting.\n\nLike the guitar’s right there, and I’ll look at it and think I should pick it up, but then I notice the dust on the case or the way the window sticks when it rains, and somehow an hour’s gone. So, yeah, I guess I don’t have much energy, even when I haven’t really done anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you no longer take pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, those things are still there, sort of, like they belong to somebody else.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and coaching gave my week some shape. Guitar too—I’d pick it up without thinking. Now I look at it and it’s like… I know it’s mine, but there’s no pull. Same with calling people back. The phone will be right there, and I’ll think, okay, just do it, and then I’m looking at the floor or the kitchen counter or wondering why the neighbor’s car alarm always has that same little chirp.\n\nMostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I sit in that small rental for hours sometimes, knowing there are things I should do, and I can’t really get started. So… yeah, I guess I don’t get much pleasure from those things now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or in a low mood for most of the day, nearly every day, even if it's not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I know you asked that already, or something close to it, and I’m not trying to dodge it. I just don’t know if “down most of the day” is the right way to put it.\n\nLike yesterday, I spent a long time just sitting there, but I wasn’t thinking, “I’m sad.” I was noticing the mark on the wall near the outlet, and then I remembered this old place I lived in where the paint bubbled up after a leak, and then I started wondering if landlords ever really fix anything properly. So the day kind of goes sideways.\n\nIt’s not a clean yes or no for me. I can say things feel flat or heavy sometimes, but whether that’s nearly every day, most of the day… I haven’t been keeping track like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or in a low mood for most of the day, nearly every day, even if it's not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure how to answer that cleanly. “Low mood” sounds like something I’d recognize if it was sitting right in front of me, you know?\n\nSome days I’m just… there. I’m not crying or anything. I’ll make coffee and then forget it on the counter, and then I’ll notice the little ring it leaves, and that reminds me of this desk I had years ago at work—someone carved initials into it, and I always wondered who they were. Then half the morning is gone.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as down for most of the day. Things feel off, kind of dull, but I haven’t really been measuring it like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that your mood has been low or down for most of the day, on nearly every day, even if it’s not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, I do. I just… I can’t pin it down that way.\n\n“Most of the day, nearly every day” feels like those reports we had to fill out where you needed exact times, and if you didn’t write it down right away it all got muddy. My days get muddy like that. I’ll think, okay, how was my mood today, and then I’m remembering the old duty roster board at work, the smell of the dry-erase markers, who always took the blue one even though it barely wrote…\n\nSo, no, I don’t know if I can say it’s low most of the day nearly every day. Things feel dull or far away sometimes, but I haven’t noticed it in that exact way.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had days where your mood felt low or down for most of the time, and if this has happened on nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that, not the two weeks part. I get what you mean—like, if it’s been most of the day and happening almost every day—but I haven’t been marking it out like that.\n\nThere are days that feel sort of low, or just slowed down, but then I’ll lose track of whether that was the whole day or just the afternoon. Like I’ll look at the clock and remember this old clock we had in the station that always ran a few minutes fast, and everyone argued about whether to fix it or just remember the difference… and then I’m off thinking about that instead of answering.\n\nSo, I’m not saying yes. I’m just not sure. I haven’t noticed it clearly as “nearly every day for two weeks.”\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can give you a clear yes on that. I keep trying to line it up in my head—most of the day, most days, two weeks in a row—and it sort of slips around.\n\nThere have been low stretches, I guess, but then I start thinking, was that the whole day or just after lunch? Or was it because the apartment was too quiet? Like when I used to come off a long shift and the house had that still feeling, and even the refrigerator sounded loud… that kind of sticks with you. I remember one time the porch light kept flickering and I kept meaning to replace it, but then I’d forget until I saw it again at night.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed it clearly enough to say it happened most days for two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there were times when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days over a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking basically the same thing, and I’m trying not to dodge it. I just don’t have it lined up that way in my head.\n\nThere are times I feel… not good, kind of flat or heavy, but whether it was most of the day, and then most days for two weeks—those pieces don’t stay in order for me. I start counting days and then I remember little things instead, like the calendar at work where somebody would circle training days in red marker, and if the marker bled through the paper you couldn’t tell what month it was from. That’s kind of how it feels when I try to look back.\n\nSo I can’t really say yes to that. I haven’t noticed it clearly enough to say it was over two weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve lost interest in most or all of the activities you used to enjoy, even things you really liked doing before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not in a dramatic way, exactly, but mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—not like I was great, but I liked it. I’d call people back, too, or at least I meant to. Now I just… don’t. The guitar sits there, and I’ll look at it and think about how the strings probably need changing, and then I remember this little music shop that used to smell like wood and dust, and the guy there always kept picks in a coffee mug by the register… and then I’m just sitting there thinking about that instead of playing.\n\nSome days I’m in that small rental for hours, and I know there are things I should do, even things I used to want to do, but I can’t get myself to start. So, yeah, I’ve lost interest in a lot of it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you felt down or in a low mood for most of the day, and this happened nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, and I’m not trying to be difficult, but I don’t really know how to answer it cleanly.\n\nI’ve had low times, sure, where the day feels kind of gray or slowed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day” and “two weeks in a row,” I start losing track of it. I can remember pieces better than the stretch itself—like sitting at the table with the blinds half open, or noticing the same cup by the sink for a couple days, or thinking I should go outside and then not doing it.\n\nBut I can’t say for sure it was nearly every day for two weeks. I just haven’t kept track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can pin it down like that. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt low, or just kind of sunk down, but when you ask if it was most of the day, most days, for two weeks straight… I lose the thread.\n\nIt’s like, I remember little parts better than the whole stretch. I’ll remember the light in the room, or the sound of the heater clicking on, or thinking I should move something off the chair and then not doing it. But I can’t honestly say, “yes, that was two weeks in a row,” because I wasn’t tracking it that way.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I don’t have a clear yes for that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I think… I don’t know. I know you just asked it, and I’m trying to line it up in my head, but it doesn’t line up cleanly.\n\nThere were stretches where I was down, yeah, or everything felt heavy, but “most of the day” and “most days” for two weeks… I can’t really swear to that. I can’t give you a solid yes on that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days over a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know we’re circling the same thing, but I still don’t really have a clean answer for it.\n\nThere have been times I felt low, yeah, but when you put it into that shape—most of the day, most days, two weeks—I can’t tell if my memory fits that or if I’m just guessing. I tend to remember odd bits, like where I was sitting or what was on the table, and then my mind goes off to something else, like whether I locked the door or what shift I was on, and then I’ve lost the timeline.\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ll make coffee and then forget it on the counter, and then I’ll notice the little ring it leaves, and that reminds me of this desk I had years ago at work—someone carved initials into it, and I always wondered who they were. Then half the morning is gone.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as down for most of the day. Things feel off, kind of dull, but I haven’t really been measuring it like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that your mood has been low or down for most of the day, on nearly every day, even if it’s not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, I do. I just… I can’t pin it down that way.\n\n“Most of the day, nearly every day” feels like those reports we had to fill out where you needed exact times, and if you didn’t write it down right away it all got muddy. My days get muddy like that. I’ll think, okay, how was my mood today, and then I’m remembering the old duty roster board at work, the smell of the dry-erase markers, who always took the blue one even though it barely wrote…\n\nSo, no, I don’t know if I can say it’s low most of the day nearly every day. Things feel dull or far away sometimes, but I haven’t noticed it in that exact way.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had days where your mood felt low or down for most of the time, and if this has happened on nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that, not the two weeks part. I get what you mean—like, if it’s been most of the day and happening almost every day—but I haven’t been marking it out like that.\n\nThere are days that feel sort of low, or just slowed down, but then I’ll lose track of whether that was the whole day or just the afternoon. Like I’ll look at the clock and remember this old clock we had in the station that always ran a few minutes fast, and everyone argued about whether to fix it or just remember the difference… and then I’m off thinking about that instead of answering.\n\nSo, I’m not saying yes. I’m just not sure. I haven’t noticed it clearly as “nearly every day for two weeks.”\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can give you a clear yes on that. I keep trying to line it up in my head—most of the day, most days, two weeks in a row—and it sort of slips around.\n\nThere have been low stretches, I guess, but then I start thinking, was that the whole day or just after lunch? Or was it because the apartment was too quiet? Like when I used to come off a long shift and the house had that still feeling, and even the refrigerator sounded loud… that kind of sticks with you. I remember one time the porch light kept flickering and I kept meaning to replace it, but then I’d forget until I saw it again at night.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed it clearly enough to say it happened most days for two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there were times when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days over a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking basically the same thing, and I’m trying not to dodge it. I just don’t have it lined up that way in my head.\n\nThere are times I feel… not good, kind of flat or heavy, but whether it was most of the day, and then most days for two weeks—those pieces don’t stay in order for me. I start counting days and then I remember little things instead, like the calendar at work where somebody would circle training days in red marker, and if the marker bled through the paper you couldn’t tell what month it was from. That’s kind of how it feels when I try to look back.\n\nSo I can’t really say yes to that. I haven’t noticed it clearly enough to say it was over two weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve lost interest in most or all of the activities you used to enjoy, even things you really liked doing before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not in a dramatic way, exactly, but mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—not like I was great, but I liked it. But then sometimes I’m not really thinking “I’m sad,” I’m just sitting there, and the day gets away from me. Like I’ll notice the neighbor’s dog barking or the mail truck, and then I’m thinking about how my old route used to have this one house with three dogs that all had different barks—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track hour by hour. It comes and goes, maybe, or I just don’t notice it the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, for most of the day, you feel down or in a low mood?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that exactly. “Most days, most of the day” feels… too neat, I guess. I have low parts, yeah, or blank parts, but I don’t always know what to call them.\n\nLike yesterday I sat by the window and watched this guy try to parallel park for, I don’t know, ten minutes, and I was thinking about how people in the city just keep tapping bumpers like it’s normal. Now I’ll be in that little rental place and there are things I know I should do, like basic stuff, and I just sit there for hours. Not even exactly sleeping. Just sitting.\n\nLike the guitar’s right there, and I’ll look at it and think I should pick it up, but then I notice the dust on the case or the way the window sticks when it rains, and somehow an hour’s gone. So, yeah, I guess I don’t have much energy, even when I haven’t really done anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you no longer take pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, those things are still there, sort of, like they belong to somebody else.\n\nI used to hike a lot, and coaching gave my week some shape. Guitar too—I’d pick it up without thinking. Now I look at it and it’s like… I know it’s mine, but there’s no pull. Same with calling people back. The phone will be right there, and I’ll think, okay, just do it, and then I’m looking at the floor or the kitchen counter or wondering why the neighbor’s car alarm always has that same little chirp.\n\nMostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore. I sit in that small rental for hours sometimes, knowing there are things I should do, and I can’t really get started. So… yeah, I guess I don’t get much pleasure from those things now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or in a low mood for most of the day, nearly every day, even if it's not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I know you asked that already, or something close to it, and I’m not trying to dodge it. I just don’t know if “down most of the day” is the right way to put it.\n\nLike yesterday, I spent a long time just sitting there, but I wasn’t thinking, “I’m sad.” I was noticing the mark on the wall near the outlet, and then I remembered this old place I lived in where the paint bubbled up after a leak, and then I started wondering if landlords ever really fix anything properly. So the day kind of goes sideways.\n\nIt’s not a clean yes or no for me. I can say things feel flat or heavy sometimes, but whether that’s nearly every day, most of the day… I haven’t been keeping track like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or in a low mood for most of the day, nearly every day, even if it's not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure how to answer that cleanly. “Low mood” sounds like something I’d recognize if it was sitting right in front of me, you know?\n\nSome days I’m just… there. I’m not crying or anything. I’ll make coffee and then forget it on the counter, and then I’ll notice the little ring it leaves, and that reminds me of this desk I had years ago at work—someone carved initials into it, and I always wondered who they were. Then half the morning is gone.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as down for most of the day. Things feel off, kind of dull, but I haven’t really been measuring it like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that your mood has been low or down for most of the day, on nearly every day, even if it’s not intense sadness?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, I do. I just… I can’t pin it down that way.\n\n“Most of the day, nearly every day” feels like those reports we had to fill out where you needed exact times, and if you didn’t write it down right away it all got muddy. My days get muddy like that. I’ll think, okay, how was my mood today, and then I’m remembering the old duty roster board at work, the smell of the dry-erase markers, who always took the blue one even though it barely wrote…\n\nSo, no, I don’t know if I can say it’s low most of the day nearly every day. Things feel dull or far away sometimes, but I haven’t noticed it in that exact way.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had days where your mood felt low or down for most of the time, and if this has happened on nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say that, not the two weeks part. I get what you mean—like, if it’s been most of the day and happening almost every day—but I haven’t been marking it out like that.\n\nThere are days that feel sort of low, or just slowed down, but then I’ll lose track of whether that was the whole day or just the afternoon. Like I’ll look at the clock and remember this old clock we had in the station that always ran a few minutes fast, and everyone argued about whether to fix it or just remember the difference… and then I’m off thinking about that instead of answering.\n\nSo, I’m not saying yes. I’m just not sure. I haven’t noticed it clearly as “nearly every day for two weeks.”\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can give you a clear yes on that. I keep trying to line it up in my head—most of the day, most days, two weeks in a row—and it sort of slips around.\n\nThere have been low stretches, I guess, but then I start thinking, was that the whole day or just after lunch? Or was it because the apartment was too quiet? Like when I used to come off a long shift and the house had that still feeling, and even the refrigerator sounded loud… that kind of sticks with you. I remember one time the porch light kept flickering and I kept meaning to replace it, but then I’d forget until I saw it again at night.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed it clearly enough to say it happened most days for two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there were times when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days over a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking basically the same thing, and I’m trying not to dodge it. I just don’t have it lined up that way in my head.\n\nThere are times I feel… not good, kind of flat or heavy, but whether it was most of the day, and then most days for two weeks—those pieces don’t stay in order for me. I start counting days and then I remember little things instead, like the calendar at work where somebody would circle training days in red marker, and if the marker bled through the paper you couldn’t tell what month it was from. That’s kind of how it feels when I try to look back.\n\nSo I can’t really say yes to that. I haven’t noticed it clearly enough to say it was over two weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve lost interest in most or all of the activities you used to enjoy, even things you really liked doing before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not in a dramatic way, exactly, but mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nI used to hike, and I coached for a while, and I’d play guitar—not like I was great, but I liked it. I’d call people back, too, or at least I meant to. Now I just… don’t. The guitar sits there, and I’ll look at it and think about how the strings probably need changing, and then I remember this little music shop that used to smell like wood and dust, and the guy there always kept picks in a coffee mug by the register… and then I’m just sitting there thinking about that instead of playing.\n\nSome days I’m in that small rental for hours, and I know there are things I should do, even things I used to want to do, but I can’t get myself to start. So, yeah, I’ve lost interest in a lot of it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you felt down or in a low mood for most of the day, and this happened nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, and I’m not trying to be difficult, but I don’t really know how to answer it cleanly.\n\nI’ve had low times, sure, where the day feels kind of gray or slowed down, but when you say “most of the day” and “nearly every day” and “two weeks in a row,” I start losing track of it. I can remember pieces better than the stretch itself—like sitting at the table with the blinds half open, or noticing the same cup by the sink for a couple days, or thinking I should go outside and then not doing it.\n\nBut I can’t say for sure it was nearly every day for two weeks. I just haven’t kept track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can pin it down like that. I don’t have a clear yes for that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I think… I don’t know. I can’t give you a solid yes on that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a time when your mood felt low or down for most of the day, and this happened on most days over a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know we’re circling the same thing, but I still don’t really have a clean answer for it.\n\nThere have been times I felt low, yeah, but when you put it into that shape—most of the day, most days, two weeks—I can’t tell if my memory fits that or if I’m just guessing.
